# Codex audit: Phase 5.7 V2.1 — Transport binding (LoopbackPair foundations)

## Context

Phase 5.7 V2.1 lands the first Transport-surface bindings on top of V1's
CollabSession-only binding. V1 closed clean (Engine HEAD `89dd5f0d170`, IDE
HEAD `97e0513d134`) with 3-way megaudit + 5 HIGH findings closed.

V2.1 scope: Loopback foundations + core attach/flush/poll. NO async (deferred
to V2.3+), NO multi-window demo, NO WebSocket binding.

## Targets

**Engine repo** (`quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/` on `feat/quantbook-engine`):

- `crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs` (lines ~715-745): new
  `attach_transport_boxed(&mut self, Box<dyn Transport + Send>)` method.
  The generic `attach_transport<T>` now delegates to it. Plus 2 new
  Rust unit tests at `crates/ql-collab/src/session.rs` tests module.
- `crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs`: new `Transport` napi class + new
  `LoopbackPair` napi class + 5 new `CollabSession` napi methods
  (`attachTransport`, `detachTransport`, `hasTransport`, `flushToTransport`,
  `pollRemote`) + new Send compile-assert for `Transport` + new engine
  round-trip Rust test.

**IDE repo** (`quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/` on `feat/visualise-v1`):

- `src/quantbook/types.ts`: new `TransportInstance`, `LoopbackPairInstance`,
  `LoopbackPairConstructor` interfaces + 5 new methods added to
  `CollabSessionInstance`.
- `src/quantbook/session.ts`: new `loopbackTransportPair()` +
  `createLoopbackPair()` helpers.
- `test/quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts`: 14 new mocha tests in a new
  "quantbook V2.1 -- Transport binding" suite.

Tests: engine workspace 4461+ / 0 (running gate); IDE mocha 40/40 passing.
fmt + clippy clean.

## Audit scope — focus areas

1. **`attach_transport_boxed` correctness**: Does the boxed sibling preserve
   ALL invariants of the generic `attach_transport<T>` (VV baseline reset,
   replacement semantics, no double-free or use-after-move)? The generic
   now delegates to the boxed version — verify the delegation is correct.

2. **`Transport` opaque class consumption semantics**: The napi class uses
   `Option<Box<dyn CoreTransport + Send>>` + a `take_inner()` Rust-only
   method. After `CollabSession::attachTransport` moves the box out, the
   wrapper is consumed. Audit:
   - Can a malicious or buggy caller revive a consumed wrapper?
   - Is the error message ("already been consumed") actionable?
   - What happens when a consumed wrapper is dropped on the JS side?
   - Are there race conditions if two threads (impossible in V8, but
     check Rust-side too) call `take_inner` simultaneously?

3. **`LoopbackPair` single-use takers**: `take_a` / `take_b` each
   consumable once via `Option::take`. Verify:
   - Order independence (takeA then takeB ≠ takeB then takeA in behavior).
   - What happens if a pair is dropped before both takes? (Both ends
     of the Loopback queue should clean up.)
   - Does the `Default` impl interact correctly with `#[napi(constructor)]`?

4. **CollabSession transport method error mapping**: Each new `#[napi]`
   method maps engine errors to JS Error via `format!("{e}")`. Verify:
   - No path that swallows or transforms a fatal engine error into
     `Ok(())`.
   - `flush_to_transport` returning `Ok(false)` on no-transport is correct
     (not an error condition — the engine treats it as a no-op).
   - `poll_remote` returning `Ok(0)` on no-transport is correct.
   - The error mapping uses `format!("{e}")` which loses Display
     formatting nuances; is there a precision loss that hurts IDE-side
     diagnosis?

5. **napi-rs class instance lifetime**: `Transport` and `LoopbackPair` are
   `#[napi]` classes. Their JS-side lifetime is managed by V8's GC. Verify:
   - Rust drop order is sane when JS GC runs late vs early.
   - No dangling references when a Transport is moved out of its wrapper
     and the wrapper is GC'd before the session attaches.
   - The Box<dyn Transport + Send> ownership chain is unambiguous.

6. **Rule 4 application**: The new `Transport` class has a `Send`
   compile-assert. Verify the `!Sync` claim is correct (or document it
   as "not claimed"). Per audit-discipline memory, V1 had 3 Rule 4
   violations — sweep for any new ones in V2.1.

7. **IDE TS interface alignment**: `types.ts` declares 4 new
   interfaces. Audit each method signature matches the napi return
   types exactly (return type narrowness, optional-vs-required,
   Promise-wrapping where applicable — V2.1 has no async methods).

8. **Test coverage**:
   - The 14 new mocha tests cover happy-path + error-path. Are there
     missed edge cases?
   - The Rust round-trip test in `ql-bindings-node` uses
     `CoreCollabSession` directly (napi types can't be tested in
     `cargo test` due to link issues). Are there Rust-side cases
     this misses that mocha doesn't catch either?
   - **pollRemote returns BLOB count, not op count** — this was a
     real semantic gotcha found during test writing. Verify all docs +
     tests correctly reflect this.

9. **Multi-attach edge cases**: What happens if a session has an
   attached transport AND another `attachTransport` call comes in
   with a fresh Transport? The Rust side replaces and returns the
   prior (dropped on the napi side per V2.1 design). Verify:
   - Background tasks of the prior transport (none for Loopback, but
     V2.3+ WebSocketTransport will have them) are released cleanly.
   - The new transport's baseline reset is correct.

10. **Documentation consistency**: V2.1 introduces multiple new
    docstrings. Cross-check that:
    - The "single-use" semantics are documented uniformly
      (Transport.isAttachable, LoopbackPair.takeA/takeB, error
      messages).
    - The `pollRemote` blob-vs-op-count semantic is documented in
      types.ts + session.ts + engine napi method docstring.

## Output format

Standard verdict: PASS / PASS-WITH-FINDINGS / FAIL.
Categories: HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW / OBS, each with:
- Location (file:line if possible)
- What the finding is
- Why it matters
- Recommended closure (if any)

Reference prior V1 / megaudit transcripts only if relevant to the V2.1
finding — V1 is closed; this audit is V2.1-specific.

# VS Code Copilot Instructions

## Project Overview

Visual Studio Code is built with a layered architecture using TypeScript, web APIs and Electron, combining web technologies with native app capabilities. The codebase is organized into key architectural layers:

### Root Folders
- `src/`: Main TypeScript source code with unit tests in `src/vs/*/test/` folders
- `build/`: Build scripts and CI/CD tools
- `extensions/`: Built-in extensions that ship with VS Code
- `test/`: Integration tests and test infrastructure
- `scripts/`: Development and build scripts
- `resources/`: Static resources (icons, themes, etc.)
- `out/`: Compiled JavaScript output (generated during build)

### Core Architecture (`src/` folder)
- `src/vs/base/` - Foundation utilities and cross-platform abstractions
- `src/vs/platform/` - Platform services and dependency injection infrastructure
- `src/vs/editor/` - Text editor implementation with language services, syntax highlighting, and editing features
- `src/vs/workbench/` - Main application workbench for web and desktop
  - `workbench/browser/` - Core workbench UI components (parts, layout, actions)
  - `workbench/services/` - Service implementations
  - `workbench/contrib/` - Feature contributions (git, debug, search, terminal, etc.)
  - `workbench/api/` - Extension host and VS Code API implementation
- `src/vs/code/` - Electron main process specific implementation
- `src/vs/server/` - Server specific implementation

The core architecture follows these principles:
- **Layered architecture** - from `base`, `platform`, `editor`, to `workbench`
- **Dependency injection** - Services are injected through constructor parameters
- **Contribution model** - Features contribute to registries and extension points
- **Cross-platform compatibility** - Abstractions separate platform-specific code

### Built-in Extensions (`extensions/` folder)
The `extensions/` directory contains first-party extensions that ship with VS Code:
- **Language support** - `typescript-language-features/`, `html-language-features/`, `css-language-features/`, etc.
- **Core features** - `git/`, `debug-auto-launch/`, `emmet/`, `markdown-language-features/`
- **Themes** - `theme-*` folders for default color themes
- **Development tools** - `extension-editing/`, `vscode-api-tests/`

Each extension follows the standard VS Code extension structure with `package.json`, TypeScript sources, and contribution points to extend the workbench through the Extension API.

### Finding Related Code
1. **Semantic search first**: Use file search for general concepts
2. **Grep for exact strings**: Use grep for error messages or specific function names
3. **Follow imports**: Check what files import the problematic module
4. **Check test files**: Often reveal usage patterns and expected behavior

## Validating TypeScript changes

MANDATORY: Always check the `VS Code - Build` watch task output via #runTasks/getTaskOutput for compilation errors before running ANY script or declaring work complete, then fix all compilation errors before moving forward.

- NEVER run tests if there are compilation errors
- NEVER use `npm run compile` to compile TypeScript files but call #runTasks/getTaskOutput instead

### TypeScript compilation steps
- Monitor the `VS Code - Build` task outputs for real-time compilation errors as you make changes
- This task runs `Core - Build` and `Ext - Build` to incrementally compile VS Code TypeScript sources and built-in extensions
- Start the task if it's not already running in the background

### TypeScript validation steps
- Use the run test tool if you need to run tests. If that tool is not available, then you can use `scripts/test.sh` (or `scripts\test.bat` on Windows) for unit tests (add `--grep <pattern>` to filter tests) or `scripts/test-integration.sh` (or `scripts\test-integration.bat` on Windows) for integration tests (integration tests end with .integrationTest.ts or are in /extensions/).
- Use `npm run valid-layers-check` to check for layering issues

## Coding Guidelines

### Indentation

We use tabs, not spaces.

### Naming Conventions

- Use PascalCase for `type` names
- Use PascalCase for `enum` values
- Use camelCase for `function` and `method` names
- Use camelCase for `property` names and `local variables`
- Use whole words in names when possible

### Types

- Do not export `types` or `functions` unless you need to share it across multiple components
- Do not introduce new `types` or `values` to the global namespace

### Comments

- Use JSDoc style comments for `functions`, `interfaces`, `enums`, and `classes`

### Strings

- Use "double quotes" for strings shown to the user that need to be externalized (localized)
- Use 'single quotes' otherwise
- All strings visible to the user need to be externalized using the `vs/nls` module
- Externalized strings must not use string concatenation. Use placeholders instead (`{0}`).

### UI labels
- Use title-style capitalization for command labels, buttons and menu items (each word is capitalized).
- Don't capitalize prepositions of four or fewer letters unless it's the first or last word (e.g. "in", "with", "for").

### Style

- Use arrow functions `=>` over anonymous function expressions
- Only surround arrow function parameters when necessary. For example, `(x) => x + x` is wrong but the following are correct:

```typescript
x => x + x
(x, y) => x + y
<T>(x: T, y: T) => x === y
```

- Always surround loop and conditional bodies with curly braces
- Open curly braces always go on the same line as whatever necessitates them
- Parenthesized constructs should have no surrounding whitespace. A single space follows commas, colons, and semicolons in those constructs. For example:

```typescript
for (let i = 0, n = str.length; i < 10; i++) {
    if (x < 10) {
        foo();
    }
}
function f(x: number, y: string): void { }
```

- Whenever possible, use in top-level scopes `export function x(…) {…}` instead of `export const x = (…) => {…}`. One advantage of using the `function` keyword is that the stack-trace shows a good name when debugging.

### Code Quality

- All files must include Microsoft copyright header
- Prefer `async` and `await` over `Promise` and `then` calls
- All user facing messages must be localized using the applicable localization framework (for example `nls.localize()` method)
- Don't add tests to the wrong test suite (e.g., adding to end of file instead of inside relevant suite)
- Look for existing test patterns before creating new structures
- Use `describe` and `test` consistently with existing patterns
- Prefer regex capture groups with names over numbered capture groups.
- If you create any temporary new files, scripts, or helper files for iteration, clean up these files by removing them at the end of the task
- Never duplicate imports. Always reuse existing imports if they are present.
- Do not use `any` or `unknown` as the type for variables, parameters, or return values unless absolutely necessary. If they need type annotations, they should have proper types or interfaces defined.
- When adding file watching, prefer correlated file watchers (via fileService.createWatcher) to shared ones.
- When adding tooltips to UI elements, prefer the use of IHoverService service.

   662	    /// Current number of ops in the log. Wraps [`OpLog::len`] — queries

   664	    /// `usize` field was retired because the cache became stale across
   665	    /// undo/redo). O(1) but not a free local read.
   666	    pub fn op_count(&self) -> usize {

   668	    }

   670	    /// True iff the log has no ops (session just opened, no edits yet).
   671	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   672	        self.log.is_empty()
   673	    }
   674	
   675	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** attach a `Transport`
   676	    /// implementation to this session. Subsequent
   677	    /// [`flush_to_transport`] calls push the session's current
   678	    /// snapshot through it; [`poll_remote`] drains incoming
   679	    /// bytes and merges them.
   680	    ///
   681	    /// Returns `Some(previous)` if a transport was already
   682	    /// attached and got replaced.
   683	    ///
   684	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V2 (2026-05-21):** V2 V1 was explicit-drive
   685	    /// (caller invokes `flush_to_transport` per tick). V2 V2 added
   686	    /// the opt-in [`AutoFlushPolicy::OnAppend`] for IDE callers who
   687	    /// want every mutator to propagate without scheduling their
   688	    /// own ticks. Default policy remains
   689	    /// [`AutoFlushPolicy::Disabled`] so V2 V1 callers see no
   690	    /// behavior change; switch via [`set_auto_flush_policy`].
   691	    ///
   692	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 3 (2026-05-21) — offline-write story
   693	    /// for reconnect**: this method resets `last_flushed_vv` to
   694	    /// `None` (V2 V3 step 1 contract). A new transport-peer hasn't
   695	    /// seen any of this session's ops. The next
   696	    /// [`flush_delta_to_transport`] (or any auto-flushing mutator)
   697	    /// sends from the empty VV — i.e., ALL local ops including
   698	    /// those appended while offline. **Loro's CRDT op log IS the
   699	    /// offline queue; no separate buffer needed.**
   700	    ///
   701	    /// **Idiom for explicit reattach-then-sync** (recommended for
   702	    /// callers that want the new peer to see all state
   703	    /// immediately, rather than waiting for the next mutator):
   704	    /// ```ignore
   705	    /// let _ = session.attach_transport(new_transport);
   706	    /// if session.has_pending_flush() {
   707	    ///     session.flush_delta_to_transport()?;
   708	    /// }
   709	    /// ```
   710	    /// `attach_transport` itself does NOT auto-flush — it's a
   711	    /// lifecycle event, not a mutation, and the existing API
   712	    /// returns `Option<previous>` rather than `Result<>` (changing
   713	    /// that would be a breaking V2 V1 API change). Callers needing
   714	    /// immediate sync trigger it explicitly.
   715	    pub fn attach_transport<T: Transport + Send + 'static>(
   716	        &mut self,
   717	        transport: T,
   718	    ) -> Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>> {
   719	        // **Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 1 (2026-05-21):** reset `last_flushed_vv`
   720	        // to `None`. A new transport-peer hasn't seen ANY of this
   721	        // session's ops, so the next `flush_delta_to_transport` must
   722	        // send from the empty VV (= all ops). If we kept the stale
   723	        // VV from a prior transport, the new peer would miss ops
   724	        // 0..stale_vv and end up with a corrupt view.
   725	        self.attach_transport_boxed(Box::new(transport))
   726	    }
   727	
   728	    /// **Phase 5.7 V2.1 (2026-05-22):** Box-taking sibling of
   729	    /// [`attach_transport`]. Same semantics, takes a pre-boxed
   730	    /// trait object instead of a generic.
   731	    ///
   732	    /// Exists because napi-rs cannot bind generic functions (per
   733	    /// Phase 5.7 V1 megaudit Opus-B HIGH-2). The IDE binding's
   734	    /// `Transport` opaque class extracts its inner
   735	    /// `Box<dyn Transport + Send>` and passes it here. The generic
   736	    /// `attach_transport<T>` stays as the ergonomic entry point for
   737	    /// direct Rust callers and now delegates to this method to keep
   738	    /// the baseline-reset + replacement semantics in one place.
   739	    pub fn attach_transport_boxed(
   740	        &mut self,
   741	        transport: Box<dyn Transport + Send>,
   742	    ) -> Option<Box<dyn Transport + Send>> {
   743	        // Mirror attach_transport<T>'s contract: reset the VV
   744	        // baseline so the next flush sends from empty.
   745	        self.last_flushed_vv = None;
   746	        self.transport.replace(transport)
   747	    }
   748	
   749	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19):** detach the current
   750	    /// transport. Returns it for caller cleanup; returns `None`
   751	    /// if no transport was attached.
   752	    ///
   753	    /// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Opus-A M3, 2026-05-21):**
   754	    /// the returned `Box` owns the transport's background tasks
   755	    /// (e.g., `WebSocketTransport`'s reader + writer). Drop the box

  1215	    /// V2 V1 sends the FULL snapshot each call (O(state)). V2 V3

  1223	    /// (just sends an extra idempotent snapshot). Mix-and-match
  1224	    /// is supported: set `OnAppend` for steady-state appends but

  1226	    /// operation if you want a deterministic flush point.
  1227	    pub fn flush_to_transport(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError> {

  1245	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 1 (2026-05-21):** export and send the
  1246	    /// DELTA of ops added since the last successful flush to the

  1248	    /// `Ok(false)` if no transport is attached OR if no ops have
  1249	    /// been appended since the last flush (idempotency short-
  1250	    /// circuit — closes V2 V2 audit M2/M3 echo-loop class for
  1251	    /// step 2's `poll_remote` auto-flush wiring).
  1252	    ///
  1253	    /// Wire bytes are `LoroDoc::ExportMode::Updates { from: last_flushed_vv }`.
  1254	    /// The first flush after `attach_transport` (or after `new`)
  1255	    /// sends from the empty VV — equivalent to `all_updates()` —
  1256	    /// delivering the full history to the new transport-peer.
  1257	    /// Subsequent flushes send only the delta since the prior flush.
  1258	    ///
  1259	    /// Loro's `LoroDoc::import` transparently consumes both
  1260	    /// `Snapshot` and `Updates` blobs, so the wire format is opaque
  1261	    /// to peers — switching from snapshots to deltas is a
  1262	    /// SENDER-SIDE optimization with NO PEER-SIDE CHANGES required.
  1263	    ///
  1264	    /// # Mix-and-match with `flush_to_transport`
  1265	    ///
  1266	    /// Both APIs maintain the SHARED `last_flushed_vv` field, so
  1267	    /// callers can interleave full snapshots and deltas freely. A
  1268	    /// typical pattern:
  1269	    /// - Initial handshake: `flush_to_transport` to seed the peer
  1270	    ///   with full state.
  1271	    /// - Steady state under `OnAppend`: `maybe_auto_flush` (via
  1272	    ///   `flush_delta_to_transport`) sends tiny deltas per
  1273	    ///   mutation.
  1274	    /// - On peer reconnect: caller may choose to `flush_to_transport`
  1275	    ///   again as a safety net (Loro deduplicates on import; no
  1276	    ///   harm even if the peer already has those ops).
  1277	    ///
  1278	    /// # Error handling
  1279	    ///
  1280	    /// Same partial-state contract as `flush_to_transport`:
  1281	    /// - `CollabSessionError::OpLog` on `export_delta_bytes` failure.
  1282	    /// - `CollabSessionError::Transport` on `send` failure (Closed, Io).
  1283	    /// - `last_flushed_vv` is NOT updated on `Err` — a retry sends
  1284	    ///   the same delta the failed call would have sent.
  1285	    ///
  1286	    /// # Delivery semantics — queued vs acked
  1287	    ///
  1288	    /// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Codex M1 + Opus-B M1,
  1289	    /// 2026-05-21):** "successful flush" means
  1290	    /// [`Transport::send`] returned `Ok` — which, per the trait
  1291	    /// contract, means bytes are **queued for send** to the currently-
  1292	    /// attached transport. For buffered async impls
  1293	    /// (`WebSocketTransport`), the bytes sit in an internal mpsc
  1294	    /// channel until the background writer task pushes them to the
  1295	    /// WebSocket sink. **`last_flushed_vv` advances at queue-success,
  1296	    /// not at wire-delivery confirmation.**
  1297	    ///
  1298	    /// Concrete consequences:
  1299	    /// - `has_pending_flush() == false` AFTER a successful flush means
  1300	    ///   "queued to the currently-attached transport," NOT "the peer
  1301	    ///   has received the ops."
  1302	    /// - If the transport is dropped between queue and wire (e.g., tab
  1303	    ///   close, app shutdown, transport panic), the in-flight bytes
  1304	    ///   are lost silently and the peer never receives them.
  1305	    /// - Recovery from transport drop is the same as recovery from
  1306	    ///   `Err(Closed)`: detach + reattach a new transport. The V2 V3

  1308	    ///   redelivered, including the previously-"flushed" ones the
  1309	    ///   prior transport never put on the wire.

  1311	    /// For IDE consumers building "safe to close window?" workflows,

  1317	    /// TCP-level or peer-application-level ack, build a custom ack-op

  1319	    pub fn flush_delta_to_transport(&mut self) -> Result<bool, CollabSessionError> {
  1320	        if self.transport.is_none() {
  1321	            return Ok(false);
  1322	        }

  1325	        // Closes V2 V2 audit M2/M3 (echo-loop). NOTE: VersionVector

  1354	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19, audit-tightened):** drain

  1396	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V1 (2026-05-19, audit-tightened):** like
  1397	    /// [`poll_remote`] but with an explicit per-call cap on the
  1398	    /// number of blobs to drain.
  1399	    ///
  1400	    /// `max_blobs == 0` is a no-op (returns `Ok(0)` even if
  1401	    /// blobs are queued — call again with a non-zero cap).
  1402	    ///
  1403	    /// Returns `Ok(merged)` where `merged <= max_blobs`. If the
  1404	    /// returned count equals `max_blobs`, more blobs may still
  1405	    /// be queued — call again. If less, the queue drained
  1406	    /// (either empty or transport reported `Closed`).
  1407	    ///
  1408	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 2 (2026-05-21):** when at least one
  1409	    /// blob was drained AND [`auto_flush_policy`] is `OnAppend`,
  1410	    /// fires ONE auto-flush after the loop (per-batch, not
  1411	    /// per-blob — bandwidth efficient). Routed through
  1412	    /// [`flush_delta_to_transport`]; V2 V3 step 1's idempotency
  1413	    /// guard short-circuits when the post-merge VV equals the
  1414	    /// pre-poll `last_flushed_vv` (i.e., the drained blobs only
  1415	    /// contained ops already known locally — Loro-deduped merge).
  1416	    ///
  1417	    /// **Auto-flush firing precision (V2 V4 V1 step 4 audit closures,
  1418	    /// Codex L1 + Codex L2, 2026-05-21)**: the precise rule is
  1419	    /// "after a non-empty drain that exits without error" — three
  1420	    /// normal exit paths fire the post-loop `maybe_auto_flush`:
  1421	    /// (a) loop reaches `max_blobs` cap → `while merged < max_blobs`

  1425	    /// `break` (treated as graceful EOF).
  1426	    /// If a later blob's `merge_bytes` errors (Loro decode failure)
  1427	    /// or `try_recv` errors with `Err(TransportError::Io(_))` (the
  1428	    /// only non-Closed `TransportError` variant today; future
  1429	    /// `#[non_exhaustive]` additions are similarly treated) after
  1430	    /// earlier blobs already merged, `current_vv` has advanced
  1431	    /// (earlier merges committed) but the post-loop
  1432	    /// `maybe_auto_flush` is SKIPPED via the `?` early return. This
  1433	    /// is NOT a false-synced state: `last_flushed_vv` is unchanged
  1434	    /// → `has_pending_flush()` returns `true` → next
  1435	    /// `flush_delta_to_transport` (or next `poll_remote_with_limit`
  1436	    /// that reaches a normal exit) WILL send the accumulated delta.
  1437	    /// Recovery is automatic; just bounded by when the next flush
  1438	    /// path actually fires.
  1439	    ///
  1440	    /// **Partial-state contract on auto-flush failure**: if the
  1441	    /// post-batch auto-flush fails (closed transport, I/O error
  1442	    /// during the send), the method returns

  1444	    /// blobs have been committed to `self.log`. The caller never
  1445	    /// sees `Ok(merged)` in this case; inspect `op_count()` to
  1446	    /// determine how many merges actually committed locally.
  1447	    /// `last_flushed_vv` is NOT advanced on flush Err — a retry
  1448	    /// (e.g., manual [`flush_delta_to_transport`] after detaching
  1449	    /// and reattaching a working transport) sends the same delta the
  1450	    /// failed call would have.
  1451	    pub fn poll_remote_with_limit(
  1452	        &mut self,
  1453	        max_blobs: usize,
  1454	    ) -> Result<usize, CollabSessionError> {
  1455	        let Some(transport) = self.transport.as_mut() else {
  1456	            return Ok(0);
  1457	        };
  1458	        let mut merged = 0usize;
  1459	        while merged < max_blobs {
  1460	            match transport.try_recv() {
  1461	                Ok(Some(bytes)) => {
  1462	                    self.log.merge_bytes(&bytes)?;
  1463	                    merged += 1;
  1464	                }
  1465	                Ok(None) => break,
  1466	                Err(TransportError::Closed) => {
  1467	                    // Trait contract: Closed only after queue drains. Any
  1468	                    // already-drained bytes are accounted in `merged`.
  1469	                    // Treat as graceful end-of-stream.
  1470	                    break;
  1471	                }
  1472	                Err(other) => return Err(CollabSessionError::Transport(other)),
  1473	            }
  1474	        }

  1476	        // the drain batch. ONE flush per call (not per-blob) —
  1477	        // bandwidth-efficient. V2 V3 step 1's idempotency guard at
  1478	        // `flush_delta_to_transport` returns Ok(false) without
  1479	        // invoking transport.send when no state advanced (Loro-
  1480	        // deduped merge). Skip when 0 blobs drained: state didn't
  1481	        // change locally, no point firing the VV-clone+compare
  1482	        // path (defensive optimization).
  1483	        if merged > 0 {
  1484	            self.maybe_auto_flush()?;
  1485	        }
  1486	        Ok(merged)
  1487	    }

  2595	            s.has_pending_flush(),
  2596	            "boxed reattach resets VV baseline; flush is pending again"
  2597	        );
  2598	    }

  2602	        use crate::transport::NoopTransport;
  2603	        let mut s = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1)).unwrap();
  2604	        s.attach_transport(NoopTransport::new());

   102	/// Errors emitted by `Transport` impls.
   103	#[derive(Debug, Error)]
   104	#[non_exhaustive]
   105	pub enum TransportError {
   106	    /// The underlying I/O failed (socket dead, network partition,
   107	    /// etc.). Implementations should distinguish recoverable
   108	    /// (reconnect) from permanent (auth rejected) failures via
   109	    /// variant structure in their own error type, then map to this
   110	    /// variant at the trait boundary if they don't want to expose
   111	    /// implementation details.
   112	    #[error("transport I/O error: {0}")]
   113	    Io(String),
   114	
   115	    /// The transport is closed and no further sends are accepted.
   116	    /// Receivers should still drain any already-queued bytes via
   117	    /// `try_recv` before considering the channel done.
   118	    #[error("transport closed")]
   119	    Closed,
   120	}

   122	/// Wire-byte channel for Phase 5 collaboration.
   123	///
   124	/// Implementations push and pull opaque byte blobs. The blobs ARE
   125	/// Loro `LoroDoc::export(...)` snapshots / updates; the
   126	/// transport doesn't interpret them. Framing (where one blob ends
   127	/// and the next starts) is the implementation's responsibility.
   128	pub trait Transport {
   129	    /// Push `bytes` to the channel. Implementations may buffer
   130	    /// internally; the call returns when the bytes are
   131	    /// **queued for send**, not when the remote has received them.
   132	    ///
   133	    /// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Codex M1 + Opus-B M1,
   134	    /// 2026-05-21):** for buffered impls (`WebSocketTransport` and
   135	    /// future async transports), there is a window between "send
   136	    /// returned Ok" and "bytes on the wire" where the bytes can be
   137	    /// silently lost (transport dropped, peer disconnect mid-flush,
   138	    /// allocator failure during the writer task). The session's
   139	    /// [`crate::CollabSession::flush_delta_to_transport`] advances
   140	    /// `last_flushed_vv` immediately on `send`'s Ok return, so
   141	    /// `has_pending_flush() == false` alone means "queued to the
   142	    /// currently-attached transport," NOT "the peer has received the
   143	    /// ops." **V2 V4 V1 step 1 (2026-05-21) Tier K1 closure**: call
   144	    /// [`Transport::flush_pending`] after `flush_delta_to_transport`
   145	    /// to block until the writer task has completed `ws_sink.send`
   146	    /// for every queued blob — that provides level-1 (local writer)
   147	    /// ack. For stronger guarantees (peer-application ack, TCP-level
   148	    /// ack), keep the transport attached until you've verified peer
   149	    /// reception out-of-band (e.g., via a custom ack-op layered on
   150	    /// `merge_bytes`). On transport drop or `Err(Closed)`, detach +
   151	    /// reattach a new transport — the V2 V3 step 1 baseline-reset
   152	    /// contract re-sends from empty VV.
   153	    ///
   154	    /// On error, the caller should treat the byte blob as unsent
   155	    /// and re-queue (or surface the error to the user). The
   156	    /// transport will not retry automatically — that policy lives
   157	    /// at the layer above (Phase 5.5 design will pick reconnect
   158	    /// semantics).
   159	    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError>;
   160	
   161	    /// Non-blocking receive. Returns `Some(bytes)` if a blob is
   162	    /// queued, `None` otherwise. Implementations choose how often
   163	    /// they poll the underlying channel; the contract is "if you
   164	    /// have something, give it to me, else `None`."
   165	    ///
   166	    /// Returns `Err(TransportError::Closed)` only when the channel
   167	    /// is permanently closed AND its internal queue is empty. A
   168	    /// transient I/O failure during poll surfaces as
   169	    /// `Err(TransportError::Io)`.
   170	    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError>;
   171	
   172	    /// Read the most recent transport-internal error, if any. Default
   173	    /// returns `None` for impls without a runtime-error concept
   174	    /// (`NoopTransport`, `LoopbackTransport`). Buffered async impls
   175	    /// (`WebSocketTransport`) override to expose the underlying cause
   176	    /// of the most recent task-observed failure.
   177	    ///
   178	    /// **V2 V3 step 5 megaudit closure (Opus-A H1, 2026-05-21):** the
   179	    /// V2 V3 step 4 closure added `WebSocketTransport::last_error()`
   180	    /// on the concrete type for IDE consumers driving reconnect
   181	    /// handshakes — but `CollabSession::attach_transport` moves the
   182	    /// concrete type into `Box<dyn Transport + Send>`, making it
   183	    /// unreachable. Lifting the accessor to the trait + adding
   184	    /// [`crate::CollabSession::transport_last_error`] proxy is the
   185	    /// minimum-viable fix: IDE callers can now distinguish "peer
   186	    /// reset" from "auth rejected" from "capacity exceeded" without
   187	    /// downcasting or holding a parallel handle.
   188	    ///
   189	    /// Returns `Option<String>` (lossy) rather than a structured
   190	    /// error type to keep the trait minimal and avoid leaking
   191	    /// impl-specific types (`WebSocketError`, etc.). Consumers
   192	    /// pattern-match on substring or just display the message.
   193	    ///
   194	    /// Read AFTER observing [`TransportError::Closed`] from `send`
   195	    /// or `try_recv`. Returns `None` for clean shutdowns (caller
   196	    /// `close()`, graceful peer Close frame, transport never failed).
   197	    fn last_error(&self) -> Option<String> {
   198	        None
   199	    }
   200	
   201	    /// Block until every byte blob previously queued via `send` has
   202	    /// been written to the underlying transport's wire (e.g. WebSocket
   203	    /// sink). Default `Ok(())` for synchronous transports
   204	    /// (`LoopbackTransport`, `NoopTransport`) where `send` is already
   205	    /// wire-delivery — there is no buffer to drain.
   206	    ///
   207	    /// **Phase 5.5 V2 V4 V1 step 1 (2026-05-21) — Tier K1 ack channel.**
   208	    /// Closes the V2 V3 step 5 megaudit's convergent finding (Codex M1
   209	    /// and Opus-B M1): buffered async transports
   210	    /// (`WebSocketTransport`) return `Ok` from `send` once bytes are
   211	    /// queued in an mpsc channel, NOT when bytes reach the wire.
   212	    /// Without `flush_pending`, `CollabSession::has_pending_flush()
   213	    /// == false` would mean "queued to currently-attached transport,"
   214	    /// not "delivered." For IDE consumers building "safe to close
   215	    /// window?" workflows, this is a real UX hole. `flush_pending`
   216	    /// lets callers block until the local writer has caught up,
   217	    /// providing a level-1 ack (bytes hit `ws_sink.send`
   218	    /// successfully). TCP-ack (level 2) and peer-application-ack
   219	    /// (level 3) require lower-layer hooks or custom protocols
   220	    /// respectively.
   221	    ///
   222	    /// # Errors
   223	    ///
   224	    /// - `Err(TransportError::Closed)` if the transport is closed
   225	    ///   (either before `flush_pending` was called OR during the wait
   226	    ///   — e.g., writer task failed mid-flush and set the closed
   227	    ///   flag).
   228	    /// - `Err(TransportError::Io)` for internal synchronization
   229	    ///   failures (mutex poisoning from a panicked task).
   230	    ///
   231	    /// # Async-context caveat
   232	    ///
   233	    /// `flush_pending` is blocking-sync (uses `Condvar::wait_timeout`).
   234	    /// Calling it from within a tokio task body will block the
   235	    /// runtime worker. Wrap with `tokio::task::block_in_place` (on
   236	    /// multi-thread runtimes) or `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` (on
   237	    /// any runtime) to avoid stalling other tasks.
   238	    fn flush_pending(&mut self) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
   239	        Ok(())
   240	    }
   241	}
   242	
   243	/// No-op `Transport` impl for tests + scaffolding.
   244	///
   245	/// Records every `send` call in an internal `Vec` (so tests can
   246	/// assert what would have gone over the wire) and `try_recv`
   247	/// returns `None` forever (no incoming bytes). Suitable for tests
   248	/// that exercise `CollabSession::append_op` without needing a
   249	/// real channel. For 2-peer round-trip tests, use
   250	/// [`LoopbackTransport::pair`] instead — `NoopTransport` is for
   251	/// "I just need a Transport-shaped object" cases.
   252	#[derive(Debug, Default)]
   253	pub struct NoopTransport {
   254	    /// Bytes the consumer would have pushed to the wire. Tests
   255	    /// inspect this to verify their `CollabSession` calls emit the
   256	    /// expected number / shape of exports.
   257	    pub sent: Vec<Vec<u8>>,
   258	    closed: bool,
   259	}

   293	/// **Phase 5.5 V1 (2026-05-19):** in-process paired `Transport`
   294	/// impl for 2-peer round-trip testing.
   295	///
   296	/// Constructed via [`LoopbackTransport::pair`]: returns two
   297	/// endpoints `(a, b)` where `a.send(bytes)` lands in `b`'s recv
   298	/// queue and vice versa. The two endpoints share two
   299	/// `Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>` channels via interior
   300	/// mutability — `Send + Sync` so they can also be used across
   301	/// threads if a future test wants that.
   302	///
   303	/// ## Use case
   304	///
   305	/// Wire two `CollabSession`s together for tests like:
   306	/// ```ignore
   307	/// let (mut tx_a, mut tx_b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
   308	/// let session_a = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(1))?;
   309	/// let session_b = CollabSession::new(PeerId::new(2))?;
   310	/// // ... append ops on a, drain via tx_a.send / tx_b.try_recv ...
   311	/// ```
   312	///
   313	/// **Phase 5.5 V2 V2 (2026-05-21):** `CollabSession` supports
   314	/// opt-in auto-flush via `AutoFlushPolicy::OnAppend` — every
   315	/// session mutator (`append_op`, `merge_bytes`, presence writes,
   316	/// `undo` / `redo` when consumed, `sweep_presence`) then sends
   317	/// through the attached transport automatically. Default policy
   318	/// remains `Disabled` (V2 V1 explicit-drive behavior). Tests can
   319	/// still drive the flow manually: append, export_bytes, send via
   320	/// the transport, recv on the other side, merge_bytes — useful
   321	/// when the test wants deterministic control over when bytes
   322	/// land on the wire (e.g. ordering tests). **Phase 5.5 V2 V3 step 2
   323	/// (2026-05-21):** `poll_remote*` now also triggers auto-flush after
   324	/// a non-empty drain (one flush per call, not per-blob). See
   325	/// [`crate::AutoFlushPolicy::OnAppend`] for the receive-side
   326	/// contract.
   327	///
   328	/// ## Close semantics
   329	///
   330	/// Each endpoint has its OWN `closed` flag (separate
   331	/// `AtomicBool`). `a.close()` shuts down endpoint A:
   332	/// - A's `send` returns `TransportError::Closed` immediately.
   333	/// - A's `try_recv` drains any already-queued bytes FIRST and
   334	///   only returns `Closed` once the queue is empty (per the
   335	///   `Transport` trait contract at the trait docstring).
   336	/// - B is unaffected: B can still `send` (bytes land in A's
   337	///   inbox; A drains them on the next try_recv before reporting
   338	///   Closed) and `try_recv` (drains B's inbox).
   339	///
   340	/// ## Atomic ordering caveat
   341	///
   342	/// `close` uses `AtomicBool` with `Relaxed` ordering. For
   343	/// cross-thread "close then send" semantics, callers must use
   344	/// external synchronization — a thread that observes
   345	/// `is_closed() == true` after another thread's `close()` is NOT
   346	/// guaranteed by this transport alone (the atomic only protects
   347	/// the flag itself, not the surrounding sequence).
   348	pub struct LoopbackTransport {
   349	    /// Channel WE drain via `try_recv`. The peer's `send` writes
   350	    /// here.
   351	    inbox: Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>,
   352	    /// Channel WE write to via `send`. The peer's `try_recv`
   353	    /// drains here.
   354	    outbox: Arc<Mutex<VecDeque<Vec<u8>>>>,
   355	    /// Per-endpoint close flag. Atomic so `close(&self)` doesn't
   356	    /// need `&mut`.
   357	    closed: AtomicBool,
   358	}
   359	
   360	impl std::fmt::Debug for LoopbackTransport {
   361	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
   362	        f.debug_struct("LoopbackTransport")
   363	            .field(
   364	                "inbox_len",
   365	                &self.inbox.lock().map(|q| q.len()).unwrap_or(0),
   366	            )
   367	            .field(
   368	                "outbox_len",
   369	                &self.outbox.lock().map(|q| q.len()).unwrap_or(0),
   370	            )
   371	            .field("closed", &self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
   372	            .finish()
   373	    }
   374	}
   375	
   376	impl LoopbackTransport {
   377	    /// Construct a pair of `LoopbackTransport` endpoints. Returns
   378	    /// `(a, b)` where `a.send(...)` enqueues to `b.try_recv()` and
   379	    /// `b.send(...)` enqueues to `a.try_recv()`.
   380	    pub fn pair() -> (Self, Self) {
   381	        let a_to_b = Arc::new(Mutex::new(VecDeque::new()));
   382	        let b_to_a = Arc::new(Mutex::new(VecDeque::new()));
   383	        let a = Self {
   384	            inbox: b_to_a.clone(),
   385	            outbox: a_to_b.clone(),
   386	            closed: AtomicBool::new(false),
   387	        };
   388	        let b = Self {
   389	            inbox: a_to_b,
   390	            outbox: b_to_a,
   391	            closed: AtomicBool::new(false),
   392	        };
   393	        (a, b)
   394	    }
   395	
   396	    /// Mark this endpoint closed. Subsequent `send` / `try_recv`
   397	    /// calls return `TransportError::Closed`. The peer endpoint
   398	    /// is NOT affected by this call (each side has its own close
   399	    /// flag).
   400	    pub fn close(&self) {
   401	        self.closed.store(true, Ordering::Relaxed);
   402	    }
   403	
   404	    /// True if this endpoint has been closed.
   405	    pub fn is_closed(&self) -> bool {
   406	        self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed)
   407	    }
   408	
   409	    /// Number of bytes blobs waiting in this endpoint's inbox
   410	    /// (i.e. sent by the peer, not yet drained via `try_recv`).
   411	    /// Useful for tests that want to assert "the peer sent N
   412	    /// blobs to me" without consuming them.
   413	    pub fn pending_recv(&self) -> usize {
   414	        self.inbox.lock().map(|q| q.len()).unwrap_or(0)
   415	    }
   416	}

   418	impl Transport for LoopbackTransport {
   419	    fn send(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), TransportError> {
   420	        if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   421	            return Err(TransportError::Closed);
   422	        }
   423	        self.outbox
   424	            .lock()
   425	            .map_err(|e| TransportError::Io(format!("loopback outbox lock poisoned: {e}")))?
   426	            .push_back(bytes.to_vec());
   427	        Ok(())
   428	    }
   429	
   430	    fn try_recv(&mut self) -> Result<Option<Vec<u8>>, TransportError> {
   431	        // Trait contract (transport.rs:71-74): "Closed only when the
   432	        // channel is permanently closed AND its internal queue is
   433	        // empty." So drain any already-queued bytes first; only
   434	        // return Closed when both closed AND drained.
   435	        // Codex + Opus 5.5 V1 audit MEDIUM/HIGH closure: pre-closure
   436	        // we returned Closed immediately on close, violating the
   437	        // contract.
   438	        let mut queue = self
   439	            .inbox
   440	            .lock()
   441	            .map_err(|e| TransportError::Io(format!("loopback inbox lock poisoned: {e}")))?;
   442	        if let Some(bytes) = queue.pop_front() {
   443	            return Ok(Some(bytes));
   444	        }
   445	        // Queue empty — now distinguish "open but empty" from "closed".
   446	        if self.closed.load(Ordering::Relaxed) {
   447	            Err(TransportError::Closed)

     1	//! `ql-bindings-node` — VS Code extension binding (Phase 5.7 V1).
     2	//!
     3	//! **Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22, this ship):** binds `CollabSession` for the
     4	//! quantlab VS Code fork's extension host. Replaces the Phase-6+ reserved
     5	//! placeholder this crate used to be.
     6	//!
     7	//! ## Architecture
     8	//!
     9	//! cdylib + napi-rs 3.x. The `#[napi]` derive macros generate the
    10	//! JS-facing class shape from the Rust struct. napi-rs handles:
    11	//!   - JS class lifecycle (`new`, finalize)
    12	//!   - `BigInt <-> u64` conversion for `PeerId`
    13	//!   - `Uint8Array <-> &[u8]`: `as_ref()` exposes a slice INTO the
    14	//!     JS-owned ArrayBuffer (verified via napi-rs 3.9.0
    15	//!     `arraybuffer.rs:653`'s `slice::from_raw_parts` on the SAB-or-AB
    16	//!     backing store). On export we hand ownership of a `Vec<u8>` to
    17	//!     JS via `Uint8Array::from`, which transfers the buffer rather
    18	//!     than copying.
    19	//!   - panic safety: **napi-rs 3.x does NOT wrap entry points in
    20	//!     `catch_unwind` by default** (Phase 5.7 V1 audit Codex H1 +
    21	//!     Opus H3, 2026-05-22 — verified by reading napi-derive-backend
    22	//!     5.0.4 `src/codegen/fn.rs:217-240`: the wrap is gated on
    23	//!     `#[napi(catch_unwind)]` opt-in). The V1 binding does NOT use
    24	//!     `catch_unwind` — instead, every `#[napi]` method
    25	//!     pre-validates inputs that could trigger engine-side `assert_*!`,
    26	//!     `panic!`, `unwrap`, or `expect`. Smoke caught one such hazard
    27	//!     (PeerId(0) → `CollabSession::new`'s `assert_ne!(peer, 0)` →
    28	//!     Node abort "failed to initiate panic, error 5"); the
    29	//!     `peer_id_from_bigint` helper now pre-rejects 0. V2+ each new
    30	//!     `#[napi]` method MUST sweep the engine for FFI-reachable
    31	//!     assertions and pre-validate the same way (see V2 backlog).
    32	//!
    33	//! ## V1 surface
    34	//!
    35	//! Single class `CollabSession` exposing the minimum methods that let
    36	//! TS code drive a round-trip:
    37	//!   - `new(peerId: bigint)`: wraps `ql_collab::CollabSession::new`
    38	//!   - static `fromSnapshot(peerId: bigint, bytes: Uint8Array)`:
    39	//!     wraps `from_snapshot`
    40	//!   - `appendPutValue(sheet, row, col, value)`: convenience for V1
    41	//!     (single Op variant; full Op enum binding deferred to V2)
    42	//!   - `exportBytes() -> Uint8Array`: snapshot
    43	//!   - `mergeBytes(bytes: Uint8Array) -> number`: returns merged op count
    44	//!   - `opCount() -> number`
    45	//!   - `pendingOpCount() -> number` (V2 V4 V1 step 2 helper)
    46	//!   - `hasPendingFlush() -> boolean` (V2 V3 step 3 helper)
    47	//!   - `peerId() -> bigint`
    48	//!
    49	//! ## V1 deferred (see `docs/phase5/5-7-v1-exit-packet.md`)
    50	//!
    51	//! Full V1-deferred-to-V2/V3 list lives in the V1 exit packet's
    52	//! "V1 deferred to V2" table. Summary: Transport binding
    53	//! (LoopbackTransport, WebSocketTransport, `attach_transport` /
    54	//! `detach_transport` / `has_transport` / `flush_to_transport` /
    55	//! `flush_delta_to_transport` / `flush_pending_to_transport` /
    56	//! `poll_remote` / `poll_remote_with_limit` /
    57	//! `transport_last_error` / `set_auto_flush_policy`), multi-window
    58	//! demo, undo/redo (UndoGroupGuard RAII translation), presence
    59	//! (PresenceState shape), full Op enum (beyond PutValue),
    60	//! Format/D-1 (FormatId enum), `rebuild_workbook` (D-3
    61	//! production-visible closure — Phase 5.7 V3), `discard_pending_ops`,
    62	//! `.qbook` persistence import/export, `#[napi(catch_unwind)]` opt-in
    63	//! for defense in depth, SharedArrayBuffer defensive copy, BigInt
    64	//! return for `opCount`/`pendingOpCount`/`mergeBytes`,
    65	//! `mergeBytesDelta` companion, `@napi-rs/cli` publish pipeline,
    66	//! Windows-specific `.dll` naming, CI `QUANTBOOK_REQUIRE_ENGINE=1`
    67	//! enforcement. V2 picks up Transport; V3 picks up cell-grid UI +
    68	//! persistence + `rebuild_workbook` wiring.
    69	//!
    70	//! ## Send + Sync
    71	//!
    72	//! `CollabSession` is `Send + !Sync` (verified per V2 V4 V1 step 3 +
    73	//! audit-discipline Rule 4). napi-rs's generated class instance lives
    74	//! in a `Box<CollabSession>` stored in JS object slot via `napi_wrap`
    75	//! (verified by reading napi-rs 3.9.0
    76	//! `src/bindgen_runtime/callback_info.rs:90-126`: NO `Send`/`Sync`
    77	//! bound on the wrapped type). napi-rs does NOT lock the instance.
    78	//! Safety against concurrent `&mut self` rests on (Phase 5.7 V1 audit
    79	//! Opus M1 closure, 2026-05-22):
    80	//!   1. JS event loop is single-threaded per Worker / main thread.
    81	//!   2. `napi::Reference<T>` is NOT Send (intentional, per napi-rs
    82	//!      docs: "drop must run on the same thread as creation"). This
    83	//!      compile-time prevents passing the class reference to a
    84	//!      different Worker thread.
    85	//!   3. `napi::Reference<T>: Sync` only when `T: Sync`. `CollabSession:
    86	//!      !Sync` blocks even read-only cross-thread sharing.
    87	//!
    88	//! Conclusion: `Send + !Sync` is the correct bound; the IDE cannot use
    89	//! this class concurrently from multiple Workers even if it wanted to.
    90	//! The prior docstring claimed "napi internal locking" -- that was
    91	//! wrong (no such lock exists); the actual safety is via 1+2+3 above.
    92	
    93	#![deny(clippy::all)]
    94	#![allow(clippy::missing_safety_doc)] // napi-rs generated wrappers

   106	/// **Phase 5.7 V1 (2026-05-22):** smoke method exposing the binding's
   107	/// own version. The IDE's loader uses this to verify the loaded
   108	/// `.node` file matches the engine version it expects. V2 will add a
   109	/// proper version-mismatch error path.
   110	#[napi]
   111	pub fn version() -> String {
   112	    env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION").to_string()
   113	}
   114	
   115	/// **Phase 5.7 V1 megaudit closure (Codex HIGH, 2026-05-22):**
   116	/// Validate a JS Number argument that represents a u32-domain index
   117	/// (row, col). Rejects non-finite, negative, fractional, and out-of-u32
   118	/// values with a distinct error message naming the parameter.
   119	///
   120	/// Why this exists: napi-rs's `napi_get_value_uint32` (used for `u32`
   121	/// params) applies ECMAScript `ToUint32`, silently coercing `-1` to
   122	/// `u32::MAX`, `NaN`/`Infinity` to `0`, fractions to floor-toward-zero.
   123	/// By taking the param as `f64` (raw `napi_get_value_double`) and
   124	/// validating here, we surface bad inputs as explicit JS Errors
   125	/// instead of corrupting workbook state.
   126	fn validate_u32_index(name: &str, value: f64) -> Result<u32> {
   127	    if !value.is_finite() {
   128	        return Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
   129	            "appendPutValue: {name} must be a finite non-negative integer, got {value}"
   130	        )));
   131	    }
   132	    if value < 0.0 {
   133	        return Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
   134	            "appendPutValue: {name} must be a non-negative integer, got {value}"
   135	        )));
   136	    }
   137	    if value.fract() != 0.0 {
   138	        return Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
   139	            "appendPutValue: {name} must be an integer, got {value}"
   140	        )));
   141	    }
   142	    if value > u32::MAX as f64 {
   143	        return Err(Error::from_reason(format!(
   144	            "appendPutValue: {name} must be in [0, 4294967295] (u32::MAX), got {value}"
   145	        )));
   146	    }
   147	    Ok(value as u32)
   148	}
   149	
   150	/// Helper: convert a JS `BigInt` to `PeerId` with explicit rejection of
   151	/// FIVE failure modes:
   152	///   - negative BigInt (signed bit set) — PeerId is u64-domain
   153	///   - BigInt doesn't fit in u64 (lossless = false)
   154	///   - `peer_id == 0` — Loro's LEGACY_PEER sentinel; rejected with a
   155	///     PROACTIVE check here (the engine's `CollabSession::new` would
   156	///     hit `assert_ne!` and panic, which napi-rs 3.x doesn't reliably
   157	///     catch into a JS exception — observed during V1 smoke test:
   158	///     "failed to initiate panic, error 5, aborting"). Pre-validate
   159	///     in the FFI boundary to keep Node alive.
   160	///   - `peer_id == u64::MAX` — Loro's `PeerID::MAX` sentinel (verified
   161	///     via Loro 1.12 `loro-internal-1.12.0/src/loro.rs:184`:
   162	///     `if peer == PeerID::MAX { return Err(...) }`). Loro returns a
   163	///     CLEAN `Err` here (not a panic), so the FFI boundary is intact
   164	///     either way — but pre-rejecting at the binding gives a faster
   165	///     failure and a consistent error message paired with the
   166	///     `peer_id == 0` case.
   167	///
   168	/// napi-rs 3.x `BigInt::get_u64()` returns `(sign_bit: bool, value: u64,
   169	/// lossless: bool)`. `sign_bit == true` indicates a negative BigInt.
   170	/// Per CLAUDE.md no-fallback rule: each failure mode surfaces a distinct
   171	/// error message so IDE consumers can show the right user-facing copy.
   172	///
   173	/// **Phase 5.7 V1 smoke-test finding (2026-05-22)**: the original
   174	/// version of this helper allowed `peer_id == 0` through, expecting
   175	/// `CollabSession::new`'s `assert_ne!(0, 0)` to surface a clean error.
   176	/// In practice the panic ABORTED the Node process. Lesson:
   177	/// engine-level `assert_*!` is NOT a safe boundary for FFI. Every
   178	/// engine method that uses `assert_*!` for input validation needs a
   179	/// proactive pre-check at the FFI layer.
   180	///

   525	///      in V2.3) and passes them to `CollabSession.attachTransport(t)`.
   526	///
   527	/// **Single-use semantics**: an instance owns its boxed trait object.
   528	/// `CollabSession.attachTransport` MOVES the box out of this wrapper,
   529	/// leaving it consumed. Subsequent attach calls with the same wrapper
   530	/// return a JS Error ("Transport has already been consumed or detached").
   531	/// This mirrors Rust ownership semantics within the constraint that JS
   532	/// doesn't have a move primitive.
   533	///
   534	/// **Why opaque (no introspection methods)**: the inner `Box<dyn Transport>`
   535	/// is a fat pointer to one of several impls (Loopback, WebSocket, ...).
   536	/// V2.1 doesn't expose impl-specific accessors; future versions can add
   537	/// `kind() -> string` if a use case emerges.
   538	#[napi]
   539	pub struct Transport {
   540	    // `Option` so we can move the inner Box out at attach time without
   541	    // dropping the wrapper. napi-rs's class instance has `&mut self`
   542	    // discipline (per V1 module docs Send+!Sync rationale) — we cannot
   543	    // consume `self` from a `#[napi]` method, only take from an Option.
   544	    inner: Option<Box<dyn CoreTransport + Send>>,
   545	}
   546	
   547	#[napi]
   548	impl Transport {
   549	    /// `true` while this wrapper still owns its inner transport (not yet
   550	    /// passed to `attachTransport`). Useful for IDE code that wants to
   551	    /// branch on whether a Transport instance is still usable without
   552	    /// catching an exception.
   553	    #[napi(js_name = "isAttachable")]
   554	    pub fn is_attachable(&self) -> bool {
   555	        self.inner.is_some()

   557	}

   559	impl Transport {

   562	    /// the Rust generic). After this returns `Some`, the wrapper is
   563	    /// consumed; subsequent `is_attachable` returns `false` and the
   564	    /// inner box has been handed off.
   565	    pub(crate) fn take_inner(&mut self) -> Option<Box<dyn CoreTransport + Send>> {
   566	        self.inner.take()

   569	
   570	/// Two-ended in-process Transport pair (LoopbackTransport).
   571	///
   572	/// JS callers use this as a stepping-stone to obtain the two `Transport`
   573	/// instances that share a Loopback queue:
   574	///
   575	/// ```ignore
   576	/// const pair = new LoopbackPair();
   577	/// const transportA = pair.takeA();
   578	/// const transportB = pair.takeB();
   579	/// sessionA.attachTransport(transportA);
   580	/// sessionB.attachTransport(transportB);
   581	/// ```
   582	///
   583	/// **Why this intermediate class** (Phase 5.7 V2.1 design note,
   584	/// 2026-05-22): napi-rs's `#[napi(factory, ...)]` can only return a
   585	/// single `Self`-typed class instance. Returning a 2-tuple or
   586	/// `Vec<Transport>` fails the trait bound `ObjectFinalize` napi-rs
   587	/// imposes on factory returns. The cleanest pattern that keeps each
   588	/// `Transport` end as a separately-attachable class is to expose the
   589	/// pair-creation step as its own short-lived class with two takers.
   590	///
   591	/// **Single-use takers**: each of `takeA` / `takeB` can be called once
   592	/// per `LoopbackPair` instance. Calling a second time on the same end
   593	/// returns a JS Error. (Calling a non-yet-taken end after the other has
   594	/// been taken still works — the two ends are independent.)
   595	#[napi]
   596	pub struct LoopbackPair {
   597	    // Both ends live here until taken. The pair is constructed eagerly
   598	    // in `new()` so the shared queue exists before either end is
   599	    // distributed.
   600	    a: Option<LoopbackTransport>,
   601	    b: Option<LoopbackTransport>,
   602	}
   603	
   604	impl Default for LoopbackPair {
   605	    fn default() -> Self {
   606	        Self::new()
   607	    }
   608	}
   609	
   610	#[napi]
   611	impl LoopbackPair {
   612	    /// Construct a fresh Loopback pair. Both ends start un-taken.
   613	    #[napi(constructor)]
   614	    pub fn new() -> Self {
   615	        let (a, b) = LoopbackTransport::pair();
   616	        Self {
   617	            a: Some(a),
   618	            b: Some(b),
   619	        }
   620	    }
   621	
   622	    /// Take ownership of end A. Errors if already taken.
   623	    #[napi(js_name = "takeA")]
   624	    pub fn take_a(&mut self) -> Result<Transport> {
   625	        let end = self.a.take().ok_or_else(|| {
   626	            Error::from_reason("LoopbackPair.takeA already called on this pair".to_string())
   627	        })?;
   628	        Ok(Transport {
   629	            inner: Some(Box::new(end)),
   630	        })
   631	    }
   632	
   633	    /// Take ownership of end B. Errors if already taken.
   634	    #[napi(js_name = "takeB")]
   635	    pub fn take_b(&mut self) -> Result<Transport> {
   636	        let end = self.b.take().ok_or_else(|| {
   637	            Error::from_reason("LoopbackPair.takeB already called on this pair".to_string())
   638	        })?;
   639	        Ok(Transport {
   640	            inner: Some(Box::new(end)),
   641	        })
   642	    }
   643	}

   420	    /// IDE has no use for the opaque prior transport, and exposing it

   437	        // The Option<Box> it returns is the PRIOR transport; we drop
   438	        // it here intentionally (see method docstring).
   439	        let _prior = self.inner.attach_transport_boxed(boxed);
   440	        Ok(())
   441	    }
   442	
   443	    /// Detach the currently-attached transport. Returns `true` if one
   444	    /// was attached (now released), `false` if there was nothing to
   445	    /// detach.
   446	    ///
   447	    /// **Background-task lifecycle (V2 V3 step 5 closure)**: detaching
   448	    /// a `WebSocketTransport` (V2.3+) drops its reader + writer tasks.
   449	    /// V2.1's LoopbackTransport has no background tasks; detach is
   450	    /// pure memory release.
   451	    ///
   452	    /// The returned `Box<dyn Transport>` is dropped Rust-side. JS does
   453	    /// NOT receive the prior transport — same rationale as
   454	    /// `attachTransport`.
   455	    #[napi(js_name = "detachTransport")]
   456	    pub fn detach_transport(&mut self) -> bool {
   457	        self.inner.detach_transport().is_some()
   458	    }
   459	
   460	    /// `true` iff a transport is currently attached.
   461	    #[napi(js_name = "hasTransport")]
   462	    pub fn has_transport(&self) -> bool {
   463	        self.inner.has_transport()
   464	    }
   465	
   466	    /// Full-snapshot flush to the attached transport. Sends the entire
   467	    /// op log as bytes. Returns `true` if bytes were actually sent.
   468	    ///
   469	    /// **Use `flushDeltaToTransport` instead in production** —
   470	    /// full-snapshot flushes get expensive as the op log grows. V2.1
   471	    /// exposes this method primarily for tests and "initial sync"
   472	    /// scenarios; V2.2 ships the delta path.
   473	    ///
   474	    /// **Failure modes**:
   475	    /// - No transport attached → returns `Ok(false)` (NOT an error).
   476	    /// - Transport's `send` returns `Err(TransportError::*)` → JS Error.
   477	    #[napi(js_name = "flushToTransport")]
   478	    pub fn flush_to_transport(&mut self) -> Result<bool> {
   479	        self.inner
   480	            .flush_to_transport()
   481	            .map_err(|e| Error::from_reason(format!("{e}")))
   482	    }
   483	
   484	    /// Drain inbound bytes from the attached transport (single-pass).
   485	    /// Returns the number of ops merged (clamped to `u32::MAX`).
   486	    ///
   487	    /// V2.1 ships the un-limited variant. `pollRemoteWithLimit` lands
   488	    /// in V2.2 (next cycle).
   489	    ///
   490	    /// **Auto-flush integration (V2 V3 step 2)**: when AutoFlushPolicy

   494	    /// V2.2 does.

   496	    /// **Failure modes**:

   498	    /// - Transport's `try_recv` returns `Err` → JS Error.
   499	    /// - Merging the bytes returns Err → JS Error.
   500	    #[napi(js_name = "pollRemote")]
   501	    pub fn poll_remote(&mut self) -> Result<u32> {
   502	        let merged = self
   503	            .inner
   504	            .poll_remote()
   505	            .map_err(|e| Error::from_reason(format!("{e}")))?;
   506	        Ok(u32::try_from(merged).unwrap_or(u32::MAX))
   507	    }
   508	}
   509	
   510	// =============================================================
   511	// Phase 5.7 V2.1 (2026-05-22) — Transport binding
   512	// =============================================================
   513

   524	///      (`Transport.loopbackPair()` here; `Transport.websocketConnect(url)`
   525	///      in V2.3) and passes them to `CollabSession.attachTransport(t)`.

    31	 * bytes) surface as JS `Error` exceptions with the engine's error
    32	 * message string.

    54		 * the same snapshot: both calls return the same `opCount` value.
    55		 * V2 may add a `mergeBytesDelta` companion that returns just the
    56		 * newly merged count, once the use case (e.g., "warn if peer

    58		 */

    60

   102		 *

   107

   134	export interface CollabSessionConstructor {
   135		new(peerId: bigint): CollabSessionInstance;
   136	
   137		/** Reconstruct a session from a previously-exported snapshot. */
   138		fromSnapshot(peerId: bigint, bytes: Uint8Array): CollabSessionInstance;

   143	// =====================================================================

   145	/**

   147	 *
   148	 * **Single-use semantics**: an instance owns its boxed trait object.

   150	 * wrapper consumed. After consumption, `isAttachable()` returns

   155	 * V2.3+: `Transport.websocketConnect(url)`).

   161		 * future API that consumes the wrapper).

   167		 * }

   169		 */

   171	}

    10	 * The wrapper exists so:

    27	import { loadQuantbookEngine } from './loader';
    28	import type { CollabSessionInstance, LoopbackPairInstance, TransportInstance } from './types';
    29	
    30	/**
    31	 * Construct a fresh `CollabSession` for the given peer.
    32	 *
    33	 * @param peerId Non-zero u64-domain peer identifier. Throws if `0n`,
    34	 *               negative, or doesn't fit in u64.
    35	 */
    36	export function createSession(peerId: bigint): CollabSessionInstance {
    37		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
    38		return new engine.CollabSession(peerId);
    39	}
    40	
    41	/**
    42	 * Append a `PutValue` op with JS-side input validation that the napi
    43	 * layer cannot perform (due to ECMAScript ToUint32 coercion happening
    44	 * BEFORE the Rust code sees the value).
    45	 *
    46	 * **V1 audit closure (Opus H2 + Codex H3, 2026-05-22)**: napi-rs's
    47	 * `napi_get_value_uint32` applies ECMAScript ToUint32 to JS Number
    48	 * arguments. That converts:
    49	 *   - `-1` to `0xFFFFFFFF` (silent wrap)
    50	 *   - `NaN` to `0` (silent coercion)
    51	 *   - `Infinity` to `0` (silent coercion)
    52	 *   - `2.5` to `2` (silent floor-toward-zero)
    53	 *
    54	 * The Rust binding sees the post-ToUint32 value and has no way to
    55	 * detect the original JS-side intent. Validate at the TS layer
    56	 * BEFORE the call so each failure surfaces a precise error.
    57	 *
    58	 * @throws Error if sheet/row/col aren't non-negative integers in range,
    59	 *               or if value isn't a finite number.
    60	 */
    61	export function appendPutValueValidated(
    62		session: CollabSessionInstance,
    63		sheet: number,
    64		row: number,
    65		col: number,
    66		value: number,
    67	): void {
    68		if (!Number.isInteger(sheet) || sheet < 0 || sheet > 0xFFFF) {
    69			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: sheet must be an integer in [0, 65535], got ${sheet}`);
    70		}
    71		if (!Number.isInteger(row) || row < 0 || row > 0xFFFFFFFF) {
    72			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: row must be an integer in [0, 4294967295], got ${row}`);
    73		}
    74		if (!Number.isInteger(col) || col < 0 || col > 0xFFFFFFFF) {
    75			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: col must be an integer in [0, 4294967295], got ${col}`);
    76		}
    77		if (typeof value !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(value)) {
    78			throw new Error(`appendPutValue: value must be a finite number, got ${value}`);
    79		}
    80		session.appendPutValue(sheet, row, col, value);
    81	}
    82	
    83	/**
    84	 * Reconstruct a `CollabSession` from a previously-exported snapshot.
    85	 *
    86	 * @param peerId Non-zero u64-domain peer identifier for THIS session
    87	 *               (need not match the snapshot's origin peer; CRDT will
    88	 *               author new ops under this id).
    89	 * @param bytes  Snapshot bytes from another session's `exportBytes()`.
    90	 */
    91	export function sessionFromSnapshot(peerId: bigint, bytes: Uint8Array): CollabSessionInstance {
    92		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
    93		return engine.CollabSession.fromSnapshot(peerId, bytes);
    94	}

    96	/**
    97	 * Return the engine binding crate's version string. For diagnostics
    98	 * + the V2 version-mismatch error path.
    99	 */

   107	
   108	/**

   121	 *
   122	 * @example
   123	 * ```ts
   124	 * const [tA, tB] = loopbackTransportPair();
   125	 * sessionA.attachTransport(tA);
   126	 * sessionB.attachTransport(tB);
   127	 * // mutate sessionA, flushToTransport, pollRemote on sessionB
   128	 * ```
   129	 */
   130	export function loopbackTransportPair(): [TransportInstance, TransportInstance] {
   131		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
   132		const pair = new engine.LoopbackPair();
   133		return [pair.takeA(), pair.takeB()];
   134	}
   135	
   136	/**
   137	 * Construct an empty `LoopbackPair` without taking either end. Use
   138	 * when the two takes need to happen at different times.
   139	 */
   140	export function createLoopbackPair(): LoopbackPairInstance {
   141		const engine = loadQuantbookEngine();
   142		return new engine.LoopbackPair();
   143	}

    45	function engineAvailable(): { ok: true } | { ok: false; reason: string } {
    46		const enginePath = resolveEnginePath();
    47		if (!fs.existsSync(enginePath)) {
    48			return {
    49				ok: false,
    50				reason: `engine binary not found at ${enginePath} -- build with: cd ../quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine && cargo build -p ql-bindings-node --release`,
    51			};
    52		}
    53		return { ok: true };
    54	}
    55	
    56	function shouldSkip(): { skip: boolean; reason?: string } {
    57		const r = engineAvailable();
    58		if (!r.ok) {
    59			const seen = global as unknown as { __quantbookEngineSkipLogged?: boolean };
    60			if (!seen.__quantbookEngineSkipLogged) {
    61				seen.__quantbookEngineSkipLogged = true;
    62				console.warn(`[quantbook-roundtrip.test] SKIPPING: ${r.reason}`);
    63			}
    64			return { skip: true, reason: r.reason };
    65		}
    66		return { skip: false };
    67	}

   515			// <= max_blobs".)

   520			sessionB.attachTransport(tB);

   523				sessionA.appendPutValue(0, i, 0, i * 10);
   524			}

   526			sessionA.flushToTransport();

   545			// B writes, A receives.

   555			// V2 V3 step 1 contract: every attach resets `last_flushed_vv`,
   556			// so the next flush sends from empty. Verify via has_pending_flush:
   557			//   1. attach, write, flush -> hasPendingFlush=false
   558			//   2. detach (or reattach) -> hasPendingFlush=true

   571			session.attachTransport(tA2);

   592			// Behavioral spec: detaching session A's transport drops the
   593			// bytes it just sent. Peer B's poll then returns 0 (the bytes
   594			// went into the Loopback queue which was tied to the now-dropped
   595			// transport end on A's side).

   597			// NOTE: this pins current Loopback semantics. If the engine

   611			// between the two ends; A's end dropping doesn't necessarily
   612			// drain B's receive buffer). The behavioral spec we pin: B
   613			// can poll without error, and the result is either 0 or
   614			// >=1; we assert no panic and a valid u32.

    44	 * 2. V1 dev-path discovery: walk UP from `__dirname` until we find
    45	 *    the extensions/quantlab/package.json anchor (identified by
    46	 *    `name: "quantlab"`), then `..` twice to the IDE repo root, then

    54	 * package.json. That meant a user `cd`'d into the engine worktree
    55	 * would walk up to the WRONG IDE-root (the engine worktree's own) and
    56	 * resolve to `quantlab-quantbook/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/...`

   120	function findExtensionDir(start: string): string | undefined {
   121		let cur = path.resolve(start);
   122		for (let depth = 0; depth < 16; depth += 1) {
   123			const pkgPath = path.join(cur, 'package.json');
   124			if (fs.existsSync(pkgPath)) {
   125				try {
   126					const raw = fs.readFileSync(pkgPath, 'utf8');
   127					const pkg = JSON.parse(raw) as { name?: string };
   128					if (pkg.name === 'quantlab' && cur.endsWith(path.join('extensions', 'quantlab'))) {
   129						return cur;
   130					}

   132					// Malformed package.json or read failure -- keep walking.
   133					// We're scanning ancestors; don't surface a partial read.
   134				}
   135			}
   136			const parent = path.dirname(cur);
   137			if (parent === cur) {
   138				return undefined;
   139			}
   140			cur = parent;
   141		}
   142		return undefined;
   143	}

codex
The core ownership paths look straightforward so far: boxed attach is the single implementation, `Option::take()` makes wrapper consumption one-way, and Loopback endpoints are owned by Arcs with no borrowed state. I’m now checking the external contract text because the poll return semantics are the easiest place for drift.

**Verdict: PASS-WITH-FINDINGS**

**HIGH**
None.

**MEDIUM**
1. [lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:484), [types.ts](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/types.ts:123), [quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/test/quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts:501)  
   `pollRemote` is still documented/test-labeled as returning “ops merged” in the napi docstring and TS interface, while the engine returns blob count. The 3-op single-flush mocha test comments describe the correct blob semantics, but the assertion is only `>= 1`, so it would not catch a regression back to op-count semantics.  
   Why it matters: IDE callers may build incorrect progress/sync logic from the public TS contract.  
   Recommended closure: change docs to “number of blobs drained, capped by the engine default poll limit”; rename local variables/messages from `merged` to `blobsDrained`; make the multi-op single-flush test assert `strictEqual(blobsMerged, 1)`.

2. [loader.ts](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/src/quantbook/loader.ts:215)  
   The IDE loader still validates only `version()` and `CollabSession`. A stale V1 native binary without `Transport` / `LoopbackPair` passes `loadQuantbookEngine()`, then fails later as a less-actionable `TypeError` when V2.1 helpers call `new engine.LoopbackPair()`.  
   Why it matters: V2.1 added required native exports, but version/export skew is not caught at the boundary.  
   Recommended closure: validate `Transport` and `LoopbackPair` exports in `loadQuantbookEngine()`, update the expected-exports error text, and add a cache-poisoning-style test for “V1-shaped module missing V2.1 exports”.

**LOW**
1. [lib.rs](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab-quantbook/quantbook-engine/crates/ql-bindings-node/src/lib.rs:527)  
   Single-use error wording is not uniform. The doc says “consumed or detached”, while the actual error says “passed to attachTransport, or obtained from a spent LoopbackPair”; the latter is not quite accurate because a spent `LoopbackPair` throws before returning a `Transport`.  
   Recommended closure: standardize on “Transport has already been consumed by attachTransport” for `Transport`, and keep `LoopbackPair.takeA/takeB already called` for pair takers.

2. [quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts](/Users/sanzhar/Documents/Sanzhar/Sanzhar/quantlab/quantlab/extensions/quantlab/test/quantbook-roundtrip.test.ts:591)  
   The test title/comment says detach drops in-flight loopback bytes, but the assertion intentionally accepts either `0` or `>=1`. Current Arc-backed Loopback queues can preserve B’s receive buffer after A detaches.  
   Recommended closure: retitle as “detach does not make peer poll panic” or assert the exact intended queue behavior.

**OBS**
- `attach_transport_boxed` looks correct: generic attach delegates to it, it resets `last_flushed_vv`, and `Option::replace` preserves replacement/drop ownership cleanly.
- `Transport` consumption is one-way via `Option::take`; dropping a consumed wrapper drops only `None`. Safe Rust cannot call `take_inner` concurrently without external synchronization.
- `LoopbackPair.takeA/takeB` are order-independent by construction. Dropping the pair before both takes just drops untaken endpoints and their Arc refs.
- No engine errors are swallowed in the new napi methods. `flushToTransport -> false` and `pollRemote -> 0` on no transport match engine no-op semantics.
- Rule 4 is acceptable for V2.1: `Transport` has a positive `Send` assert, and I do not see a new `Sync` claim for it.

Verification note: I attempted targeted Rust tests, but this shell has no `cargo` on `PATH`, so this is a source audit only.
